The code trigger 10.0 (:1571) IS empirically grounded (quiet 2.5-4.7 / q99 ~= 8 at 305 sta; 1.85 max over 12 h at 60-sta sim; events 17.8-288 on DOY-129@305, GnssConfig.hpp:4741).
### `network.scoped_handover_response`  (SCOPE the pivot-handover response to the constellation whose datum changed; PROMOTED 2026-09-11, SPEC_event_harness §19)
- **default:** src/core/config/GnssConfigLoadNetwork.cpp:2015 (false)  — **regime:** a PIVOT clock datum (inert under the anchor-free `clock_datum_sum_to_zero` / `clock_datum_broadcast_anchored`, where handovers cannot occur) AND >= 2 constellations AND a consumer of the products (closed-loop / service / RT)  — **RECOMMEND_ON.**  scope the pivot-handover carry skip / oscillator clear / jump-test suppression to the constellation whose datum changed: the collateral constellation's re-realization is the user's metre-class shock (storm arm settled p50 0.1204 -> 0.0825, max |3D| 1.34 -> 0.20 m, hour 5 0.236 -> 0.047; fresh pair edge 1.143 -> 0.117 m) and the producer's own contrasts move < 0.5 %
- **mechanism:** SCOPE the pivot-handover response to the constellation whose clock datum actually changed.  On a pivot handover the producer skips the D2 clock/code-bias carry, clears the Q3 clock oscillator state and suppresses the code-bias jump test; OFF, it does all three NETWORK-WIDE, so a Galileo pivot change re-realizes every GPS satellite's carry and vice versa.  ON, the skip/clear/suppress applies only to the changed constellation(s) — the SH-VTEC carry skip deliberately stays global (NetworkRunContext.cpp:2153).  Every producer whose products a consumer consumes (closed-loop, service_mode, RT) wants this: the collateral constellation's re-realization is what the user sees as a metre-class shock, and the producer's own per-constellation contrasts barely move — the damage is entirely in what the WIRE tells the consumer to throw away.
- **interlock:** service_mode force-arms it UNCONDITIONALLY (GnssConfigLoadNetwork.cpp:3093), including under the anchor-free clock datums where pivot handovers cannot occur — so under service_mode it is armed-but-inert.  Requires a PIVOT datum to do anything: provably inert under network.clock_datum_sum_to_zero and network.clock_datum_broadcast_anchored, and inert on a single-constellation lineage (there is no other constellation to spare).  ⚠ FINGERPRINTED — solve-affecting, so it is fresh-convergence only: it cannot be adopted mid-chain and NO warm-resume A/B is possible (the §19.4 pair had to be a fresh-convergence pair for exactly this reason).  SIDE EFFECT, and it is a fix: the READMIT run-end clause's 'dropped at a datum change' count goes 7 -> 0 — parked re-admission entries belonging to the constellation whose datum did NOT change were being discarded, silently degrading the already-promoted network.readmit_seed_horizon_s card.
- **evidence:** MECHANISM (SPEC_event_harness §19.1, census on cbscope5's own products.txt across the 319230 -> 319260 pivot edge): the 1.30 m consumer shock at a pivot departure is NOT a datum move the consumer could transform away — it is producer-internal carry free-fall applied to the WRONG constellation.  FRESH PAIR (§19.4, runs/evseed_pivfresh_off / _scoped, 643 epochs each, one exe F4FF8EF0…, single knob; a fresh pair because the knob is fingerprinted): at the Galileo edge the GPS PB L1C step rms falls 0.704 -> 0.0033 m (to the 0.0019 control), consumer 3D at the edge 1.143 -> 0.117 m, whole-run consumer rms 0.562 -> 0.185 and max 1.99 -> 0.36; Galileo's own share is untouched (PB E L1C 0.5576 -> 0.5573) and the producer contrasts are identical.  STORM ARM (§19.5, runs/golden_sim_campaign_zwdblk_scoped = cbscope5 + this knob, 720/720, 1242 s): consumer settled p50/rms 0.1204/0.2215 -> 0.0825/0.0899, window-excluded 0.1028/0.1848 -> 0.0804/0.0853, hourly p50 h0-h3 IDENTICAL and h5 0.236 -> 0.047, the pivot-recovery window 4.68-5.40 h 0.2211 -> 0.0938, last 36 min 0.2671 -> 0.0553, settled max |3D| 1.3377 -> 0.2009, Up rms/max 0.1557/1.2164 -> 0.0752/0.1968; ZWD 0.0069 -> 0.0066, WL/EWL/L1C 99.2/100/89.9 -> 99.2/100/90.2, T4 11 = 11, W2 absent, split 0.0/0, PHASE_ANCHOR 315/78 and PB-GAUGE 462/362/100 IDENTICAL, sat-clock contrast +0.10 %, code-bias rms 0.0912 -> 0.0902, rx-DCB +0.01 %, rx-clock +0.41 %.  THE LEDGER'S OWN PRE-REGISTERED SETTLING RUN IS SATISFIED — the blast radius shrinks to the changed constellation, measured at BOTH edges.  GPS edge (319260): consumer 3D 1.338 -> 0.126 m (Up -1.216 -> -0.118), Galileo CB C1C/C7Q step rms 0.0038/0.159 -> 0.00001/0.00027 and PB E L1C/L5Q 0.447/0.649 -> 0.0012/0.0017 (all to control), the GPS clock step unchanged at +1.837 (the datum DID move and still does), cta_decouple 52 (21 E + 31 G) -> 31 (G only), consumer resets PB/CB/CLK 57/57/19 -> 30/30/10 = exactly the GPS share.  Galileo edge (321900) is symmetric: consumer 0.777 -> 0.031 m, GPS CB 0.0063/0.179 -> 0.00007/0.0020, GPS PB L1C 0.542 -> 0.045 (12x; the residual is concurrent anchor handovers / slip responses, not the datum), decouples 50 -> 21 (E only), resets 54/51/17 -> 27/24/8.  ISOLATION: the ONLY event counters that moved are cta_decouple 103 -> 52, cta_disc_landed 103 -> 52, cta_recouple 154 -> 103 and seed_held 24 -> 31; common_band / if_slip / mw / lli / anchor_handover / handover_continuous / anchor_slip_handover / handover_cold / col_removed / col_added are all identical, the CLK-R clause is identical except Sum|mu| 3.59 -> 3.57 m, and the PB-GAUGE clause is byte-identical.  SECOND DEFECT FIXED: the READMIT clause's '7 dropped at a datum change' -> 0, and Q3 trips within 300 s of a clock seed 1 -> 0.  ONLY CHANNEL WORSE: run time 1050 -> 1242 s, CONFOUNDED by a different exe — a same-exe timing pair is owed, and the knob removes work rather than adding it.  RESIDUALS: the SH-VTEC carry skip stays GLOBAL by design (NetworkRunContext.cpp:2153, the RTA-2 line says so); there is still NO run-end clock-pivot handover counter, so the handover population has to be recovered from the R records; and the FIELD arm is owed — golden_field_gps_dual is GPS-only, so the knob is structurally inert there (a documented manifest exception, NOT a pin) and it becomes armable when that lineage goes multi-constellation.
- **see:** `docs/SPEC_event_harness.md` §19 (§19.1 census, §19.4 fresh pair, §19.5 storm arm + this promotion).
### `network.handover_stransform`  (queue 1(b) — RE-EXPRESS the changed constellation's carry at a pivot handover instead of dropping it; PROMOTED 2026-09-12, SPEC_event_harness §19.6)
- **verdict:** **RECOMMEND_ON** — PROMOTED 2026-09-12 on the following record.  Every WIRE gate passes at 250–575× back to control, OFF is byte-identical across TWO source revisions, section 8.7 gate 6 never fired, all producer truth channels move within ±1.6 % of a ±3 % bar, and the pooled consumer p50, the window-excluded p50, ZWD and L1C are all better or equal with the hourly profile identical -- no channel is worse beyond noise.  The two consumer EDGE gates that failed were MIS-SPECIFIED: they demanded an improvement AT the switch epoch, which increment 0 had already flattened (the scoped baseline reads 0.129 m the epoch before the GPS edge and 0.126 m at it -- there was no shock left to remove), and the unchanged switch-epoch reset COUNT is an emission property, not a carry property: the per-satellite disc bump marks phase, code and clock together, so a clock-only re-reference still resets the consumer's ambiguities.  That is pre-registered as INCREMENT 2 and is explicitly out of scope for section 8, which states that the emitted wire still steps by the datum delta BY DESIGN.  — **default:** false (`src/core/config/GnssConfigLoadNetwork.cpp`, member `include/config/GnssConfig.hpp`) — **regime:** `on("network.scoped_handover_response") and not on("network.clock_datum_sum_to_zero") and not on("network.clock_datum_broadcast_anchored")` (no `n_sys >= 2` term: it acts on the changed constellation itself, so one suffices).
- **what it does:** at a clock-pivot handover the CHANGED constellation's carried shared means are re-expressed in the incoming datum along `NetworkSbasis::clockNullDirection` — the STRUCTURAL direction the datum builder owns (§8.1 rule 1), never hand-written, never an eigenvector — by the rank-1 update `c₀ = x_q/v_c[q]`, `x ← x − v_c·c₀`, `var ← var + v_c[i]²·var_q`; the D2 shared-carry fold is then NOT skipped for them.  Increment 0 (`scoped_handover_response`) stopped a handover invalidating the constellations whose datum did NOT move; this is the remaining half.  **Only satellite clocks are transformed** — inside the shared border every other family has a zero component (the code-bias gauge is the W6 IF pin + per-(const,signal) sum-to-zero, the phase-bias gauge is the anchor-receiver ambiguity pins), which §19.1 measured directly: at the 319260 edge the clock step is a rigid +1.8365 m while the code-bias step MEAN is 1e-6 m.  **The code-bias and phase-bias carries are merely RELEASED to fold, and that release is where most of the benefit is** (§8.4 INC-1).  ⚠ The emitted wire still steps by the datum delta BY DESIGN — producer-internal continuity, never a consumer fix, and it must not be graded as one (§8.2, §8.7 gate 5).
- **evidence:** **Gate 1 (OFF byte-identity) PASS twice** — fresh 2 h null pair `runs/evseed_stsg1_{off,false}` (knob ABSENT vs FALSE, one exe): `products.txt` sha256 identical `1729cf5a…` plus dd-frac / events / solution / closed-loop / epoch-status; and `runs/evseed_stsg2_off` reproduces increment 0's `runs/evseed_pivfresh_scoped` byte-identical (`db80fcaa…`) across a different exe AND source revision.  **Gate 2 (fresh 643-epoch pair `runs/evseed_stsg2_{off,on}`, one exe, single knob)** — run-end, invariant #4: *2 edge(s) TRANSFORMED, 52 row(s) re-expressed, **376 row(s) RETAINED**, 0 no-op, **0 self-check failure(s)**, |c₀| max 1.019227 m*.  At the CLEAN first edge (Galileo 271230, arms provably identical before it) the changed constellation's own steps collapse to control: CB C1C 0.01083 → **0.00003** (361×), CB C7Q 0.25582 → **0.00060** (426×), PB L1C 0.55734 → **0.00174** (320×, *below* its 0.00203 control); the five following epochs' consumer PB resets go 18,15,17,15,6 → **0,0,0,0,0**.  GPS edge symmetric (PB L1C 0.58319 → **0.00084** against a pre-registered ≤ 0.01).  **§8.7 gate 6 did NOT fire** — ON step rms flat at +1/+4/+14/+30 min, no geometric divergence.  Truth-neutral on a ±1 % bar: sat-clock contrast rms 1.6356 → **1.6356 (0.00 %)**, code bias 0.1445 → 0.1443, rx-DCB 1.2284 → 1.2228, rx-clock 1.8562 → 1.8482, ZWD 0.0075 → 0.0073.  On that bed the consumer *also* gains: 3D rms **0.1851 → 0.1070 (−42 %)**, max 0.3605 → 0.1719 (−52 %).  **Gate 3 (ONE storm arm `runs/golden_sim_campaign_zwdblk_sts` vs `_scoped`, 720/720, 1303 s)** — c₀ at the GPS edge is **−1.839995 m**, i.e. the transform independently re-derived the exact rigid shift §19.1 had measured off the wire.  Every wire/quality gate passes: G CB C1C/C5Q 0.00921/0.16678 → **0.00002/0.00029** (460×/575×), G PB L1C 0.19914 → **0.00079**, E PB L1C 0.40473 → **0.00096**, ZWD 0.0066 → **0.0059** (max 0.0801 → **0.0261**), L1C 90.2 → **90.4**, WL 99.2, T4 11, W2 absent, split 0.0/0, cold 78, pooled p50 0.0825 → 0.0807, hourly p50 identical h0–h4, all four truth contrasts within ±1.6 % of a ±3 % bar, **every event-kind total identical** and the CLK-R / PB-GAUGE / READMIT clauses byte-identical.  **The two consumer gates FAIL**: 3D at the GPS-edge epoch 0.126 → **0.125** against ≤ 0.05, and at the Galileo edge 0.031 → **0.036** against ≤ 0.031 — because increment 0 had already removed the shock they targeted (on the scoped baseline the GPS-edge epoch 0.126 is already *below* the 0.129 of the epoch before it, so the bar asked for an improvement on a flat channel).
- **interlock:** REQUIRES `network.scoped_handover_response` — the loader DISARMS with a warning otherwise (releasing the changed constellation's skip while the global response still invalidates every sibling does not fix the edge).  INERT and disarmed under an anchor-free clock datum (`clock_datum_sum_to_zero` / `clock_datum_broadcast_anchored`): no pivot, no handover seam.  **FINGERPRINTED with an ARMED-ONLY token** (`sts:1`) so every pre-existing snapshot fingerprint is unchanged and OFF stays byte-identical, while an ARMED run is **fresh-convergence only** — never flip it mid-chain, and an A/B against a stored snapshot is invalid (§19.4 learned that the hard way on increment 0).  Two responses deliberately NOT released, both documented: the `NetworkClockQc` jump test (its statistic is the RAW clock, which genuinely steps by the datum delta — the edge stays classified as FRAME, §8.4 INC-2's other permitted answer) and the two-state clock oscillator (`clock_model` is a documented NO-GO, so transforming it would be untested code on a dead path).  ⚠ **The switch-epoch disc declarations are UNCHANGED by design**, so the consumer still resets 30 PB + 30 CB at the edge for biases whose step rms is now 0.0008 m; those are collateral of the CLOCK disc (`cta_disc_landed` marks the satellite and `markSatDiscontinuity` bumps its phase, code AND clock counters together).  That is an EMISSION question, out of scope for §8, and the named **increment 2**.
- **owed before any promotion:** a field arm (sim-validated only, on two beds); and a decision on the question the gates actually pose — the consumer value is REDUNDANT with increment 0 on a lineage that already carries it and LARGE on one that does not, so promoting this is a judgement about whether producer wire continuity is worth carrying on its own.  Run time +4.9 % (1242 → 1303 s), single arm, not attributed.
### `network.sticky_pivot`
- **default:** src/core/config/GnssConfigLoadNetwork.cpp:1975 (false)
- **evidence:** No A/B on record. The only measurement of the cost it targets is project_s2z_realization_ab.md: 'pin pivot handover (16:41, G01 sets) injects a ~1.7 m datum-invariant per-sat kick, unsignalled (A1/A2 off); s2z crosses the same membership change at 0.14 m baseline — handover is a real cost but NOT the disease.' The Doxygen benefit claim (GnssConfig.hpp:2154-2173, 'every time a lexically lower satellite RISES the pivot flips') is a mechanism assertion with no measured handover-rate delta. ⚠ **CORRECTED 2026-08-21 — the previous "Zero configs in config/ set it" was a literal-grep artifact and is wrong by 171.** Only **3** files spell the knob out, but all three are the GOLDEN BASES (`_golden305_gps.json`, `_golden63_gps_1h_laneoff.json`, `_golden63_gps_1h_laneon.json`), so **171 configs resolve it TRUE** through `extends` — and on **84** of them it is LIVE (the other 87 are inert under `clock_datum_sum_to_zero` / `clock_datum_broadcast_anchored` / an explicit `clock_datum_satellite`, per the interlock below). ⚠ **This is an UNRECORDED PROMOTION:** it entered the golden bases in `2862318` (2026-08-05), a multi-workstream commit whose message never mentions it, and it is **not** in the §A2 F16 applied list — so a knob with "no A/B on record" is live in the flagship lineage without a promotion decision (cf. the standing no-blanket-promotion directive). **RESOLVED 2026-08-21 on user instruction: DROPPED from all three golden bases** — the corpus is now literal 0 / resolved 0 (was 3 / 171, 84 live), restoring the documented default and the no-promotion posture. The knob itself is untouched (still parsed, still default-false, `service_mode` still force-arms it under a pivot datum); only the unrecorded arming is gone. Measured cost of the drop on the base being edited (`_golden63_gps_1h_laneoff`, 61 sta, 1 h, single-knob, one exe): **byte-identical** — `products.txt` and every CSV match except the timing columns, because over that window no lexically-lower satellite rose, so sticky and lexical elected the same pivot every epoch. ⚠ That is an inertness result for a 1 h window, NOT a general one: the knob acts only AT a handover, and over that hour no lexically-lower satellite rose, so the two modes never had a decision to disagree on. A longer arm can still differ. ⚠⚠ **TRAP that invalidated the first attempt at a longer arm:** `network.max_duration_s` is a PHANTOM key — the loader reads only `processing.max_duration_s` (`GnssConfigLoad.cpp:506`), and nothing anywhere reads the `network.` spelling, yet the golden bases carry BOTH (`_golden63_gps_1h_laneoff.json:74` and `:76`, same value). Setting the `network.` one produced a silent 1 h re-run that *looked* like a 12 h arm — check `Max processing duration: … s of data` in the run log before believing any duration override. Re-measure any config population with `scripts/gnss_config_chain.py`, never a grep. SETTLING RUN (still owed before any RE-arming): single-variable 305-sta/3h A/B on the flagship base under the PIN clock datum, counting pivot handovers/hour and grading total_IF(sat) deviation-from-median across each handover epoch (per the day129 grading rule).
- **interlock:** PROVABLY INERT under clock_datum_sum_to_zero and clock_datum_broadcast_anchored (warned, :2334 and :2403). service_mode force-arms it, but ONLY when neither anchor-free clock datum is selected (:3086-3092). network.clock_datum_satellite still outranks it.
### `network.trace_dd_fractional_epoch_stride`
- **default:** src/core/config/GnssConfigLoadNetwork.cpp:572-576 (jval default 1 = every epoch); struct include/config/GnssConfigTypes.hpp:462
- **evidence:** ZERO field use: every one of the 40 shipped 305-sta dd-frac configs sets epoch_stride to 1; no config anywhere sets >1. The benefit is asserted only in code — GnssConfigTypes.hpp:462 "Fraction statistics don't need every epoch; combines with max_pairs to make a traced convergence run affordable" — with no measurement behind it, so by the audit's own rule this is UNVALIDATED. SETTLING RUN: a 50-station 3 h dd-frac arm at stride 1 vs stride 5, same exe/thread count, graded with scripts/grade_ddfrac.py on circR and med|f| over the final 60 min; promote as the standard companion to max_pairs at scale only if the stride-5 statistic lands inside the stride-1 confidence interval.
- **interlock:** Inert unless trace_dd_fractional. TRACE-ONLY — explicitly NOT folded into the warm-resume fingerprint, so it may be changed on a resume (unlike network.epoch_decimation_n, which IS estimation-relevant and IS fingerprinted).
### `network.trace_dd_fractional_ref`
- **verdict:** RECOMMEND_SCOPED (A/B'd 2026-09-06; goldens keep `"lowest"` under a documented exception) — **default:** `"lowest"` (the pre-knob rule, byte-identical); enum `NetworkDdFracRef` + `NetworkConfig` field in include/config/GnssConfigTypes.hpp, parse in src/core/config/GnssConfigLoadNetwork.cpp, election in src/core/filter/NetworkDdFrac.cpp — **regime:** `on("network.trace_dd_fractional")`
- **what it does:** picks WHICH satellite every chord of `network_dd_fractional.csv` is differenced against. `"lowest"` = the lexically-lowest satellite present at >= 2 stations. `"oldest"` = the longest CONTINUOUS presence at >= 2 stations, ties -> lexically lowest (an absence erases the age, so a returning satellite re-enters at age 1).
- **evidence:** SPEC_amb_iono §6.12/§6.13. `"lowest"` pinned G01/E02 for the whole day on the sim 24 h F3 base `runs/nonint_losblock24h_hv`, and their eclipse returns re-referenced the entire trace to newborn legs — as-traced L1C 0.283 cyc / 64.2 % at the integer vs ref-free 0.089 / 86.8 %, and the h6-h9 / h18-h22 "network-wide DD episodes" were exactly those returns. Confirmed truth-free (`scripts/dd_frac_reref.py`): R 0.610 vs uniform-null 0.430, ref-free gap -0.047. **FIELD CONTRAST — the same instrument on `runs/fld63_stk_on` (DOY-129, 63 sta, 12 h) finds NOTHING:** L1C R 0.468 vs null 0.473 / gap +0.008, L2W 0.447/0.452/+0.010, L1W 0.441/0.438/+0.006, closure at its null. The field reference already rotates on its own (8-11 switches, 3-9 satellites) with no post-switch degradation, so the DOY-129 uniform-0.25 histogram is NOT this artifact and the knob buys the field nothing.
- **A/B (2026-09-06, 6 h same-build arms `_ddref6h_low` / `_ddref6h_old`, single knob, crossing G01's h4-5 and E02's h4.3 exclusions):** STRUCTURAL PASS — `products.txt` byte-identical (the `lowest` arm was re-run from scratch and reproduced its own hash), `network_solution` / `_events` / `_epoch_status` / `_stations` byte-identical, dd-frac differs. NOT INERT: 4320 elections each, **765 differed from `lowest`**, reference switches **12 -> 6**, census shifts to the settled satellite (E03 36,381 -> 112,394 L1C rows; G02 56,050 -> 81,542). GRADE: h0-h3 and GPS h4 are IDENTICAL arms (ages tie early; h0 is ignition at 1.25-1.32 cyc and not gradeable). Divergent hours, truth-graded L1C — **E h4 0.280/69.8 % -> 0.227/73.6 %; E h5 0.334/58.9 % -> 0.195/70.3 %; G h5 0.236/74.6 % -> 0.170/84.6 %**, i.e. a 25-42 % improvement at exactly the hours where the lexically-lowest reference has just returned.
- **⚠ the pre-registered PASS FAILED, and it was the wrong bar:** §6.12.5 asked that the `oldest` arm's as-traced grade match the base's REF-FREE grade; measured, it lands **2.1-3.4x above** it (0.068 / 0.058 / 0.081). A settled reference still contributes 2-3x the ref-free residual — a single reference of ANY age is still one satellite's error in every chord. Also: a policy A/B **moves the covered population** (E h4: 7,307 chords vs 8,388; the `oldest` arm's own ref-free reads 0.095/0.111 vs 0.068/0.058 and its leg-age curve starts at 0.355 vs 0.267 cyc), so never compare the two arms' ref-free columns as if they were the same chords.
- **scope:** narrow. Arm `"oldest"` only when the AS-TRACED CSV must stand alone (a viewer, an archived file, a consumer script that does not re-reference). For any GRADE the fix is the ref-free instruments, not the policy — **an `oldest` trace is quieter, never reference-FREE**: the elected satellite still carries its own slip and re-level history into every chord. **The goldens keep `"lowest"` under a documented manifest exception** — CONTINUITY, not doubt: they are the comparison baseline, and changing which satellite their dd-frac trace references would break continuity with the entire existing record of dd-frac grades, while they are already graded ref-free (strictly better than any reference policy).
- **interlock:** Inert unless `trace_dd_fractional`. TRACE-ONLY — it changes which chords appear in one diagnostic CSV; no solve input, no state, no product — so it is fingerprint-SAFE and legal on a warm resume. The age map lives in `NetworkCarryState` and is NOT snapshotted, so a warm resume restarts ages at zero and re-elects by the tie-break; the run-end switch count makes that visible rather than silent. ⚠ A/B HYGIENE (SPEC_amb_iono §6.13.4): network traces are written CWD-relative to `./logs/`, NOT `build/x64-Release/logs/` (which holds a stale Aug-13 file) — a capture from the wrong dir makes BOTH arms copy the same old file and every trace compares IDENTICAL. Assert the SOURCE mtime is newer than the run start, and cross-check any trace-only A/B against a run-end counter: byte-identity is the expected result of a correct OFF path AND of a broken capture.

### `network.warmup_epochs`
- **default:** include/config/GnssConfigTypes.hpp:648 (warmup_epochs{0}); parsed GnssConfigLoadNetwork.cpp:659  — **regime:** Same as warmup_ura_floor_m — zero occurrences in any shipped config; inert unless warmup_ura_floor_m > 0. With it at 0 the ramp waits only for phase_leveling_warmup_epochs (default 6).
- **evidence:** No measurement. The lift rule is documented but unexercised: the ramp lifts when BOTH emitted epochs >= max(warmup_epochs, phase_leveling_warmup_epochs) AND the sigma0_hat EWMA has received >= warmup_epochs updates, OR the EWMA is structurally unavailable (emit_ura + raim + phase_leveling not all armed) (GnssConfigTypes.hpp:647, loader :1881-1888). SETTLING RUN: same cold-start closed-loop A/B as warmup_ura_floor_m — the horizon is only meaningful once a non-zero floor exists to lift.
- **interlock:** Inert unless warmup_ura_floor_m > 0. In the warm-resume fingerprint (NetworkRunContext.cpp:218). Distinct from network.raim_maturity_epochs, which drives the separate RAIM maturity gate (NetworkRaim.cpp:346) through the same networkWarmupRampLifted helper.
### `network.warmup_ura_floor_m`
- **default:** include/config/GnssConfigTypes.hpp:647 (warmup_ura_floor_m{0.0}); parsed GnssConfigLoadNetwork.cpp:657  — **regime:** Never set in ANY shipped config — grep of config/test_suite/*.json returns zero occurrences for both this and warmup_epochs. The mechanism is wired and live (NetworkRunContext.cpp:538, NetworkSolPrep.cpp:391, networkWarmupRampLifted), but no field run has exercised it.
- **evidence:** No measurement exists anywhere in the loader, Doxygen, memory, or docs — only the design assertion that 'a freshly (re)started producer advertises DEGRADED quality and a consumer down-weights/withholds instead of trusting a not-yet-converged network solution' (GnssConfigTypes.hpp:647). The loader itself classifies it as a value it will not default: 'policy numeric with no universal [default]' (GnssConfigLoadNetwork.cpp:3025, restated :3209). SETTLING RUN: closed-loop producer+consumer cold start (the existing consumer replay driver, ~33 s/arm) with emit_ura armed, A/B floor 0 vs a candidate (e.g. 2-5 m), graded on consumer 3D error over the first ~30 epochs — the claim to test is that the consumer's early error DROPS because it down-weights, not merely that the URA column changes.
- **interlock:** Requires network.emit_ura — the loader warns it is a silent no-op otherwise (GnssConfigLoadNetwork.cpp:1876-1880), and the warning is suppressed in service_mode. IS IN THE WARM-RESUME FINGERPRINT (NetworkRunContext.cpp:218) => cannot be A/B'd against a shared snapshot. On an RTB-7(a) warm resume the ramp state restores from the snapshot (a warm-resumed producer is NOT degraded).
### `network.phase_leveling_los_scale`  (F1 — the per-LOS B_eff variance scale on the leveling carry)
- **default:** false = OFF, byte-identical (factor 1.0 never applied).  Loader GnssConfigLoadNetwork.cpp:2976-2977 (ARMED info :8160, INERT warn :8181 when phase_leveling off / carry_invariant on); apply site ApplicationNetworkAssembly.cpp:2846-2998 (`PlPend` pre-pass, B_eff = injectable ambiguity columns of the same (station, sat) THIS epoch, `var_eff *= B_eff` after var + q·gap and BEFORE the floor and the VCE factor); counters NetworkRunContext.hpp:2128-2134, run-end line NetworkTeardown.cpp:1885-1902, armed-only fingerprint tag `pl:losscale` NetworkRunContext.cpp:637-642 (line numbers as of 2026-09-04, pre-F3).  — **regime:** BUILT default-OFF 2026-09-04, **VALIDATED sim 3 h the same day** (`runs/nonint_los3h` vs `runs/nonint_base3h`, quiet 60-sta wu93 bed, 360 epochs, SPEC_amb_iono_split_overcount §6.7): settled L1C med|e| **2.72 → 1.28 cyc**, round0 10.2 → 23.1 %, WL(1,2) round0 **36.5 → 87.4 %**, EWL(2,5) 97.9 → 100 %, Galileo WL(1,5) 28 → 83 %, |e|/σ **83 → 1.9** (σ nearly honest), T4 attribution erases **288 → 0**, first-inject σ ratio 0.494 → 0.588 (L1C; 0.650-0.665 on L2W/L5Q/L7Q).  Run-end counter (invariant #4): `950064 row(s) scaled over 317175 LOS group(s); B_eff histogram 1:487 2:0 3:316688 4+:0`.  **Byte identity (invariant #6) MEASURED:** sha256 of `products.txt` identical between `nonint_base3h` (2026-09-03 exe) and `nonint_base3h_f1exe` (F1 exe, knob OFF) — `1c1b60c7…29229d`, 8,601,257 bytes; ambint identical to the digit.  Landed in the SPEC's pre-registered "intermediate" row (0.5-1.5 cyc), NOT the point prediction (0.13-0.25): the remainder is the STATION-COMMON over-count (SPEC §3.1) — DD identity on los3h: iono-direction part 0.64 cyc (from 2.66), non-iono residual 0.77 (from 0.26).  **Wire grade (round 4, SPEC §6.8.4): IMPROVES accuracy on every truth-graded channel** — settled sat clock rms 1.704 → **1.437 m** (p50 1.112 → 0.832; the BEST clock of any arm, F3 included), code bias 0.814 → 0.504 m, ZWD rms 0.416 → 0.191 m (but p50 0.026 → 0.122: the 5 catastrophic stations traded for a fleet-wide 12 cm wander, |err|/σ 2.6), rx-DCB 1.587 → 1.228 m, post-fit code σ̂0 1.70 → 1.06, T3/T4 false alarms 5188 → 0, consumer settled 3D 0.414 → 0.296 m (up bias +0.19 → +0.01).  **HARMS wire continuity through the untouched PB-gauge handover ceiling** (`phase_datum_handover_max_var_m2` 1e-4 m², sized to the over-counted σ): 0/93 successors continuous, 7 settled 4–14 m PB frame steps matched 7/7 to the cold-handover tows, 1653 leveling carries erased (base 57), consumer walks 0.12 → 0.55 m in the last 40 min.  With the ceiling at 1e-2 m² (`runs/nonint_los3h_hv`) continuity is only PARTIAL and per-BAND (67/93; cold L1C 3 / L2W 7 / L5Q 12 / L7Q 4 on arcs up to 3 h old, 9 mixed families, 4 settled > 1 m steps) and the consumer is HARMED (0.535 m rms, +0.43 m up bias) — an INVALID pairing.  Posture: **SUPERSEDED on the sim bed by F3** (`phase_leveling_local_block`, validated the same day, SPEC §6.8) — F3 beats it on every channel except the satellite clock; keep as F3's counted factorization fallback; do not arm alone on a lineage whose handover ceiling is 1e-4, never pair it with a lifted ceiling.
- **evidence:** MECHANISM measured on the 24 h flagship and adversarially verified (SPEC §2-§3, §2.7): the B per-band leveling rows of one LOS are injected as INDEPENDENT rows with the per-column pure conditional variance (N_rr⁻¹)_cc (NetworkRunContext.cpp:2899-2939 harvest, NetworkCoupling.cpp:381-388 one +1 local entry each) and re-assert the same iono-direction datum B times per epoch — p_k = B·p_{k-1} + p_code, K_eff = (B+1)/(B-1) = 2 (measured 2.1 per leg), **B_fit = 3.00 ± 0.03** on 9,507 mid-run newborn chords and 2.95-3.01 on 656 T4 re-births; the exact B=3 recursion reproduces the 10-epoch σ ladder 0.499/0.555/0.574/… and excludes B=2 / B=1.  The B-dependence was confirmed on the two-band V1-posture twin `runs/nonint_dual_v1_1h` (first-inject 0.565/0.604/0.636 vs triple 0.494/0.548/0.561; predicted +0.077 shift for B=2).  Response-virgin chords carry the full error (2.72 vs 2.87), so the responses are exonerated.  Station-common residue MEASURED on the ZWD: formal σ 0.0054 m vs actual rms 0.47 m at 3 h under the diagonal carry; under F1 σ 0.050 m vs med|err| 0.10-0.14 / rms 0.16 m (|err|/σ 2.1-2.8).
- **interlock:** Exact only along the per-LOS iono direction; the station-common (rx clock / ZWD / rx-DCB) over-count is reduced ~75× → ~25× but NOT removed — and it is **DD-visible** (birth-frame inheritance, station-term slope 1.18; DD(mf)·δZWD), not "DD-invisible" as this row first said.  Must be NAMED in every report; `phase_leveling_local_block` (F3) is the exact fix and SUPERSEDES this knob when both are armed.  B_eff counts INJECTED rows only: a band skipped by relevel_free_epoch / pending-reseed / max_gap / no-carry does not count (invariant #7 edge); B_eff = 1 ⇒ factor 1.  Touches the carried prior ⇒ FINGERPRINTED (armed-only tag): fresh convergence only, never flip mid-chain.  Requires phase_leveling and carry_invariant OFF (loader warns).  Composes with `phase_leveling_var_floor_m2` (scale before the floor as built — the implementer's order; the floor is falsified as a fix anyway).  ⚠ One existing response acts differently ON: the T3/T4 RAIM pool admits the coupling rows themselves (NetworkRaim.cpp:236), so their standardized residual shrinks by 1/√B_eff — that is the 288 → 0 above, a prior-side false-alarm population removed, not a data-side change.  Sim-validated only; field arm owed (the field lineages' `K_eff(30) = 2.01` says the defect is there).  ⚠ Ceiling interaction (round 4): any honest-σ carry trips the 1e-4 m² PB-gauge handover ceiling on every successor — re-express the ceiling before arming (SPEC §7 item 15).
### `network.phase_leveling_q_amb`  (the leveling-carry process noise — the levels' MEMORY)
- **default:** 0.0 m²/s = OFF (`include/config/GnssConfig.hpp:6766`; loader `GnssConfigLoadNetwork.cpp:3098-3099`, range check + zero-q pairing banner `:8465-8486`, QC q-registration `:9294`); knob plumb `NetworkRunContext.cpp:50`; apply site `ApplicationNetworkAssembly.cpp:3023` (`var_eff = stashed var + q·gap`, BEFORE the F1 B_eff scale, the var floor and the VCE factor); seed path `ApplicationNetwork.cpp:408` (`seed_rate`).  — **regime:** `on("network.phase_leveling")`. **MEASURED AND REJECTED 2026-09-10 as the lever against the (ρ, I) split freeze** (§6.18a, §7 item 34). q = 0 makes each carried level a random CONSTANT — that is what standing invariant #1 is about, and the pairing directive (`feedback_network_nonnegotiable_configs`: a near-zero q MUST be paired with `network.cycle_slip.mw_detect`) stands. This card asked the opposite question — does LOOSENING the level let the frozen (range, iono) mis-split converge? — and the answer is no. Keep at 0.0; a nonzero q does **not** relieve the pairing (12 348 T4 arc erases per 6 h remain at q = 1e-6).
- **evidence (two single-knob arms off E0 on the slipped 60-sta campaign bed `runs/simnet60_campaign_wu93`, 2 452 truth slips / 6 h, whole-run `simnet_grade`):** `runs/golden_sim_campaign_plq7` (q 1e-7 m²/s) and `_plq6` (1e-6), configs `config/test_suite/_golden_sim_campaign_{plq7,plq6}.json`. **Both FAIL.** The knob is not inert — it **halves the T4 churn** (arc erases 22 968 → 12 348 / 12 477, −46 %), the first direct evidence that the erase tier is sensitive to level STIFFNESS — but the target channel barely moves (ZWD settled truth rms 2.098 → **1.93 / 1.96 m**), the per-station-epoch ZWD truth |residual| MEDIAN gets **worse** (p50 0.123 → 0.256 / 0.450 m) and the consumer pays **+57 %** (settled 3D p50 1.751 → 2.76 / 2.75 m). The two values are indistinguishable across a decade, i.e. past the point where the prior's own scale sets anything. **Reading the data supports:** a re-levelled arc is code-quality in VALUE but phase-stiff in the block, so it is inconsistent with geometry until ~20 min of geometry change refines it, and T4 at E0's rate (21 erases per station-hour) erases it first — the erase/re-level limit cycle of §6.17b. Softening the prior lets the level MOVE but gives it nothing to move TOWARD while arcs keep restarting, which is exactly why the erase count falls (a looser prior is a smaller standardized residual) while the plateau does not. With the var-floor half NEUTRAL, the pair is §7 item 34's **FALSIFIED ×4**; the lever named instead is the split's I half carried in-block (`network.carry_iono_in_block`, §7 item 37).
- **interlock:** **FINGERPRINTED** — the value is in the snapshot fingerprint string unconditionally (`NetworkRunContext.cpp:644`, beside `pl_on` / `pl_warmup` / `pl_max_gap_s`), so a warm resume cannot cross a change of it: fresh convergence only, and an A/B against a stored snapshot is invalid. Requires `phase_leveling`; inert under `carry_invariant`. Composes with `phase_leveling_los_scale` and `phase_leveling_var_floor_m2`, which loosen the SAME row — as built the order is `q·gap` → F1 scale → floor → VCE, so a floor that binds hides the q entirely. ⚠ Do not read the halved T4 count as a QC improvement: the erases that stop are the ones whose standardized residual the looser prior shrank, not a population that was identified and cleared.
### `network.phase_leveling_var_floor_m2`  (the leveling-carry Λ cap / lock-in guard)
- **default:** 0.0 = OFF (GnssConfigLoadNetwork.cpp:2972-2973); apply site ApplicationNetworkAssembly.cpp `pl_finish_var` (`var_eff = max(var_eff, floor)` before the vce factor and buildCplRow; after the F1 scale as built).  — **regime:** **FALSIFIED AS A FIX 2026-09-04** (`runs/nonint_vf005_3h` = 0.05 m², `runs/nonint_vf001_3h` = 0.01 m², quiet 60-sta wu93 bed, 3 h; SPEC_amb_iono_split_overcount §6.7): settled L1C med|e| **3.46 / 3.64 cyc vs the 2.72 baseline — WORSE in total** (pre-registered points were 0.27 / 0.134), round0 9.0 / 9.6 %, NON-monotone (vf005 better than vf001, which the pre-registration listed as a model refuter).  What DID follow the arithmetic: the formal DD σ floors at 0.86 / 0.42 cyc (predicted 0.89 / 0.40), the first-inject ratio stays 0.494, T4 attribution erases 288 → 0 (looser rows), WL round0 rises 36.5 → 72.5 / 52.5 %.  The DD identity split names the failure: the iono-direction part fell 2.66 → 1.48 / 1.81 cyc but a GEOMETRIC residual of 2.07 / 2.18 cyc appeared (base 0.26; slope 1.7-2.2, equal-in-cycles band ratios) — a blunt floor loosens the WHOLE row and releases the station-common / geometric direction the stiff carry had pinned through the border; measured on the ZWD too (prior loosened to 0.054 m, error 0.26-0.30 m median vs base's 0.027).  Keep as the emergency lock-in guard only; never arm it to fix the level.
- **evidence:** The two arms above.  Named in the code as the lock-in guard ("caps Λ so a slowly-biasing ambiguity can never become a rigid prior the data cannot override", Assembly:2905-2908) and in NetworkSlipQc.cpp:56 as the bound on a real-but-rejected slip's damage; never armed in any shipped config (0.0 in the 24 h flagship manifest).  Under §3 of the SPEC it is a PALLIATIVE: it caps the per-row weight but each of the B rows still asserts the datum, and the |e|/σ it leaves (4.0 / 8.3, predicted 0.2-0.8) says the σ is still optimistic.  The pre-registration's error, stated plainly (SPEC §6.7.2): §6.2 projected every row onto the iono direction, so it could not see the geometric release; the round-2 theory skeptic's own floor model (0.43 / 0.19 cyc) was wrong for the same reason.  Do not re-derive the floor.  **⚠ Falsified a SECOND time, on the SLIPPED bed and against a different mechanism (2026-09-10, §6.18a, §7 item 34):** as the levels'-memory lever against the (ρ, I) split freeze it is **NEUTRAL on every channel** — `runs/golden_sim_campaign_plvf4` (1e-4 m² = 1 cm σ) and `_plvf3` (1e-3 = 3.2 cm), single knob off E0 on `runs/simnet60_campaign_wu93`, read ZWD settled truth rms 2.089 / 2.060 m against E0's 2.098, ZWD truth |residual| p50 0.111 / 0.120 (E0 0.123), consumer settled 3D p50 1.85 / 1.89 m (E0 1.751) and T4 arc erases 22 524 / 21 429 (E0 22 968).  Where the q half of the same ladder at least moved the erase count (−46 %), the floor moves nothing: at these values it does not bind often enough to matter, and at the 2026-09-04 values it binds and releases the geometry.  Arms `config/test_suite/_golden_sim_campaign_{plvf4,plvf3}.json`.
- **interlock:** Requires phase_leveling.  Applied inside `pl_finish_var` before the M2 vce factor; with `phase_leveling_los_scale` the scale is applied first (both loosen).  Not fingerprinted in code but it changes the carried prior weight — fresh-convergence-only in any A/B.  A floor that binds does NOT pin the station-common directions (this row first said so): it loosens them without adding information — the ZWD error under vf005 is 10× base's median.  ⚠ **CORRECTION 2026-09-10: it IS fingerprinted.**  The value is in the snapshot fingerprint string unconditionally (`NetworkRunContext.cpp:664`, beside `pb_var_floor`), so the "not fingerprinted in code" clause above is wrong — a warm resume cannot cross a change of it, and an A/B against a stored snapshot is invalid.  Order as built is `q·gap` (`ApplicationNetworkAssembly.cpp:3023`) → F1 B_eff scale → this floor (`:2935`, also `NetworkRunContext.cpp:3288`) → VCE factor, so a binding floor hides `phase_leveling_q_amb` entirely.
### `network.carry_iono_in_block`  (+ `_q_m2`, `_max_gap_s`)
- **default:** false / code defaults — **MAIN** — branch `worktree-ionoblk` commit `2131a3a` (rebased from `7311ad0`), merged `5276cc8` 2026-09-10; parsed in `GnssConfigLoadNetwork.cpp` beside `network.carry_zwd_in_block`; the harvest appends the per-LOS iono columns in `NetworkRunContext.cpp::networkHarvestLocalCarries`; inject pushes the `LocalBlockCol` in `ApplicationNetworkAssembly.cpp` beside the F3 `bc` build.  On main since `5276cc8` (§7 item 38's branch-exe rule no longer applies to these keys).  — **regime:** `on("network.carry_zwd") and on("network.phase_leveling_local_block")`.  The I half of the frozen (ρ, I) split carried INSIDE the station's F3 jointly-whitened local block with process noise — one column per LOS where `carry_zwd_in_block` is one per station (iono state in metres at L1, q per epoch on Σ's diagonal, absolute value carried, column order amb → iono → ZWD).  **Motive** (§6.18c, §7 item 33/37): with the T4 erases starved the anchors' levels WALK along a 2-parameter (range, iono) direction at R² 0.993–0.996 and the consumer integrates it, so the named lever was to give the I half a temporal prior the way `carry_zwd_in_block` gave the ρ half one.
- **evidence (TWO ARMS, mechanism live in both, 2026-09-10 — SPEC_amb_iono_split_overcount §6.19a / §6.19a.1, §7 item 37):** **SLIPPED** `runs/golden_sim_campaign_zwdblk_ionoblk` vs `runs/golden_sim_campaign_zwdblk` (single knob, 6 h, `runs/simnet60_campaign_wu93`, whole-run `simnet_grade`): 671 535 iono columns carried, **0 diagonal fallbacks**; ZWD truth rms 1.188 → **1.133 m** (−5 %, gate ≤ 0.5 **FAIL**), ZWD p50 0.048 → 0.042; consumer settled p50 1.085 → **1.592 m (+47 %, gate ≤ 0.6 FAIL)**, rms 2.389 → 2.24; sat-clock contrast 11.79 → 11.08; T4 arc erases 29 613 → 28 509 on an identical 645 406-opportunity denominator.  The one channel that moves the right way is the **INTEGERS** — L1C settled ref-free med|e| 1.77 → **1.32 cyc (−26 %)**, share rounding to the true integer 21 → 25 %.  **QUIET / the mandatory B9-Q1 neutrality test** `runs/golden_sim_zwdblk_ionoblk_quiet` vs `runs/golden_sim_zwdblk_quiet` (24 h, settled h12–24): consumer 3D rms 0.0875 → 0.0890 m (+1.7 %), p50 0.0525 → 0.0555, UP rms 0.0759 → 0.0768, ZWD truth 5.6 mm both; 2.75 M columns, 0 fallbacks; T4 arc erases 159 → 408 on a bed with ZERO truth slips.  **NEUTRAL — no double-count regression and no gain.**  ⭐ **What the neutral arm settles is worth more than the row: the FOUR `project_iono_carry_8b_campaign` falsifications of a per-LOS iono carry were the DIAGONAL row's station-common over-count, NOT "carrying iono"** — exactly what §6.16.7d found for ZWD (diagonal 0.723 m vs in-block 0.0877 m consumer rms on this same quiet bed).  The in-block VEHICLE is sound; the information is not there.  Eleventh probe of the day to fail the same gates the same way (§7 item 40).
- **interlock:** Loader WARN-and-DISABLES (armed-and-inert forbidden) when `phase_leveling` is off, `carry_invariant` is on, `phase_leveling_local_block` (F3) is off, or F3 cannot build a block on the posture; HARD ERROR when co-armed with the diagonal `network.carry_iono` (mutually exclusive vehicles) and on a negative `_q_m2` / `_max_gap_s`.  **Fingerprinted** (armed-only tag, like the ZWD column's `zwd:blk`) — fresh convergence only, and an A/B against a stored snapshot is invalid.  A non-SPD block or a station-epoch with no block degrades to the existing behaviour, counted.  18 unit tests on the branch; OFF path byte-identical (`D8D94923…`).
### `network.phase_leveling_young_inflate_var_m2`  (+ `_tau_ep`, `_max_age_ep`)
- **default:** 0.0 m² / code defaults — **MAIN** — branch `worktree-ionoblk` commit `e5f20d3` (rebased from `2bf6821`), merged `5276cc8` 2026-09-10 (loader `GnssConfigLoadNetwork.cpp`, range-checked with hard errors; applied inside the F3 block build).  — **regime:** `on("network.phase_leveling_local_block")`.  AGE-AWARE variance inflation of freshly re-levelled arcs **inside** the F3 block: a level whose arc was born or re-levelled within `_max_age_ep` is carried with an extra variance `V0 = _var_m2` decaying with time constant `_tau_ep`, **congruence-scaled so the block's cross terms are kept**.  Distinct from the falsified UNIFORM loosenings (`phase_leveling_var_floor_m2`, `_q_amb`) and from arm 5b's out-of-block free legs; distinct from `phase_leveling_local_block_min_age_ep`, which EXCLUDES a young column where this one INCLUDES it with an honest variance.  Motive (§6.17b/§6.18a): a re-levelled arc is CODE-quality in VALUE but PHASE-stiff in the block.
- **evidence (TWO ARMS off the in-block control `runs/golden_sim_campaign_zwdblk`, slipped bed, 6 h, whole-run `simnet_grade`, 2026-09-10 — §6.19a):** control = ZWD truth rms 1.188 m / p50 0.048, consumer p50 1.085 / rms 2.389, contrast 11.79 m, T4 29 613.  **V0 = 0.09 m², τ 20 ep** (`_zwdblk_yng`, 1.3 M column-epochs inflated): ZWD rms → **0.823 m (−31 %)** but ZWD p50 → **0.290 (6× worse)**, consumer p50 → 1.73 and **rms → 7.91 m (3.3×, a TAIL)**, contrast → 18.6, T4 → 26 076.  **V0 = 0.01 m²** (`_zwdblk_yng03`): ZWD flat 1.175, consumer p50 1.60 / rms 2.17, contrast 19.6, T4 31 956.  **Both FAIL** the pre-registered pair (ZWD ≤ 0.5 **and** consumer p50 ≤ 0.6).  ⚠ **The method rider this arm paid for: p50 and rms disagree by design on this bed.**  V0 0.09 has the second-best ZWD **rms** in the whole eleven-probe ledger and the worst ZWD **p50** and worst consumer **rms** — a p50-only ladder would have read its tail as a win.  Quote both, always.  Reading: the inflation does what it says and the tension moves to whatever is still stiff — the satellite clocks (+58 % / +66 % contrast) and the consumer's tail.  A member of §7 item 40's family: changing how much a young arc is trusted RELOCATES the station frame's walk instead of stopping it.  **Harness Study 2 rung (SPEC_event_harness §10.6):** V0 0.09 / τ 20 on ONE unnecessary erase, own null — on a QUIET station the standing effect alone moves the other arcs 0.36 → 1.64 cyc, ZWD rms network-wide +52 %, the consumer 0.854 m; on the erase the floor is 3.3× and the frame reaction 4.2× WORSE (28 455 column-epochs inflated).  KEEP_OFF with a mechanism.
- **interlock:** Requires the F3 block; hard errors on `_var_m2 < 0`, `_tau_ep ≤ 0`, `_max_age_ep ≤ 0`.  Congruence-scaled inside the block — do NOT re-implement as a diagonal add, which is the double count these cards exist to remove.  Composes with (and is hidden by) a binding `phase_leveling_var_floor_m2`, same as `_q_amb`: order is `q·gap` → F1 scale → floor → VCE, so arm at most one of the three in an A/B.  On main since `5276cc8`.
### `network.carry_rx_clock_in_block`  (+ `_q_m2`, `_max_gap_s`, `_rate_var0_m2ps2`, `_rate_q_m2ps2`, `_jump_k`, `_isb`)
- **default:** false / sub-knobs at code defaults (`_isb` true) — **MAIN** — branch `worktree-ionoblk` commit `a558d14` (rebased from `adabae6`), merged `5276cc8` 2026-09-10 (loader `GnssConfigLoadNetwork.cpp:3427-3446`, warn-and-DISABLE interlocks `:9212-9240`; getters `GnssConfig.hpp:2459-2542`; harvested and injected beside the ZWD column; run-end clause `RX-CLK-in-block` `NetworkTeardown.cpp:2052-2068`; fingerprint tag `rxclk:blk` `NetworkRunContext.cpp:706`; 24 unit tests).  — **regime:** `on("network.phase_leveling_local_block") and not on("network.carry_invariant")`.  **KEEP_OFF** (was UNVALIDATED — closed by Study 2b on its own target event).  The station RECEIVER CLOCK carried **inside** the F3 block as a random walk about a carried DRIFT (bias + rate), jump-refused beyond `_jump_k` σ, dropped after `_max_gap_s`; `_isb` carries the per-system ISB columns with it.  Motive (SPEC_amb_iono §6.19f, §7 item 44): the receiver clock had NO temporal prior on the producer, so the ρ half of every anchor's frozen (ρ, I) split was re-drawn each epoch while the I half and the ZWD had one.
- **evidence (HARNESS STUDY 2, SPEC_event_harness §10, 2026-09-10 — ten legs, quiet 60-sta bed at ep 0665, arc S21000SIM/G10/L1C, event at +10, ON on its OWN 6 h seed `runs/evseed_rxclk6h`, each arm against its own null; `event_grade.py` + `event_frame_grade.py`):** **INERT on all three injectable events** — every OFF→ON change is at or below the ON-null-vs-OFF-null floor (frame reaction 6× below it, clock 9×, consumer 2×): slip 10 cyc 0.0370 → 0.0359 cyc at +45; arc_erase floor 0.4166 → 0.4224 (1.4 % worse); cold handover frame reaction 0.06109 → 0.06106.  Null cost network-wide: clock contrast +0.02 %, ZWD +0.53 %, consumer +0.90 %.  ACTIVE (7 320 clock cols/leg, 0 not folded, 1 jump refusal in 6 h × 60 sta) and it moves the PRODUCT (43 mm on the emitted G10 clock) without moving the ERROR.  **Why, pre-registered:** the erase level error is DISPERSIVE (L2/L1 1.285–1.307, L5/L1 1.359–1.363 vs pure-iono 1.2833/1.3391; non-dispersive would be 0.78/0.75) and equals the per-LOS iono displacement to 2–5 % — a (level, slant-iono) SPLIT error, for which a ρ-direction prior has the wrong basis.  The second prediction (a ρ memory grips the mixed cold handover) FAILED: the carried value is harvested from the previous solve and already contains the error — a memory can only stop a JUMP, and there are none to inject.  OFF frame-reaction ladder: 0.0054 cyc (real 10-cyc slip) / 0.0201 (unnecessary erase, 3.7×) / 0.0611 (cold handover at a BYSTANDER station, 11×).  No storm-bed or field arm yet.  **HARNESS STUDY 2b (SPEC_event_harness §11, 2026-09-10, branch `worktree-rxstep`):** the missing injector kind was BUILT (`network.inject` `rx_clock_step` — `+step_m` metres on every code and phase observable of every satellite at ONE station, from `tow` on: the exact dual of `sat_clock_step`) and the knob was graded on its own target event.  Eight legs, same bed / snapshot / station (S21000SIM) / +10 epoch as §10, graded on G10, each against its own arm's null; the §10 null floor reproduced to the digit (`oth_d` rms 0.001657 cyc, `clk_d` rms 0.001489 m, `cons_d` 0.0218 m).  **VERDICT: KEEP_OFF — inert on its own target event too, and slightly harmful, with a mechanism.**  **(1)** OFF absorbs the step **100.000 % in the epoch it lands** (+0, not +1) at every size tested — 0.05 m, 0.5 m, 10 m — settled residual **2–31 µm**, and *nothing* else in the network moves: the station's other arcs 7e-06…3.8e-05 cyc (**44–220× below the null floor**), ZWD ≤ 3.9e-05 m, slant iono ≤ 5.4e-05 m, emitted satellite clocks 1e-06…5e-06 m rms, consumer 0.0000 m.  The receiver clock is a free per-epoch parameter, so a range-direction step at one station is EXACTLY estimable there and leaks nowhere.  **(2)** ON is **worse** by a scale-linear **0.104 % of the step** at the event epoch — `absorb_0` **0.998962 at all three sizes** (0.5 mm / 5.2 mm / 10.4 mm) — because the carried prior fights the step with its share of the epoch's normal equations; the frame reaction grows with it (`oth_d` rms 3.8e-05 → **1.76e-04** cyc at 10 m, ZWD peak 3.9e-05 → **5.0e-03** m, consumer 0.0000 → **−0.0018** m).  All still below the null floor ⇒ *no measurable benefit, a measurable-but-sub-floor cost*.  **(3) The jump gate cannot see a realistic step.**  The new armed-only run-end census (`k·√var_pred` in **metres**) reads `k = 10.0 sigma over 3660 station-epoch test(s) => gate mean 7.9863 m, min 3.0261 m, max 300.0161 m`; the 300 m tail is the documented first-increment case and removing it puts the **settled gate at ≈ 3.1 m**.  0.5 m is **6× below** the tightest station-epoch and 0.05 m **60× below**, so the pre-registered *above the gate* arm was unreachable at the default `k`.  A pre-registered extra pair at **10 m** (33 ns — an ordinary receiver-clock jump) reached it: **exactly 1 refusal**, `n_rxclk_carry_refused` 0 → 1, gate tests 3660 → 3659 (the erased station skips its next test — both edges visible).  **(4) The response is one epoch late by construction:** the refusal fires at HARVEST, after the solve, so the epoch the step lands on has already folded the stale prior — the 10.4 mm leak at +0 happens *with* the refusal armed and the gate only cleans up from +1.  ⚠ **OPEN, invariant #7:** the refusal has a counter but **no identity** — no event row, no station, no epoch — so on a field lineage “N refusals” cannot be attributed to a receiver.
- **interlock:** Loader WARN-and-DISABLES when `phase_leveling` is off, `carry_invariant` is on, F3 is off, or the posture never builds a block.  **Fingerprinted** (armed-only `rxclk:blk`) — an ON arm cannot ride an OFF seed; fresh convergence or its own seed only.  OFF byte-identical (products.txt `3bf7831b…` on `_golden_sim_off60`; the warm-resumed OFF legs hash to main's `evleg_null` / `evleg_slip`).  Its target event, a receiver-clock STEP, **was** the injector kind the harness lacked (SPEC_event_harness §8); `network.inject` `rx_clock_step` was built on branch `worktree-rxstep` (§11) and the knob was graded on it — see the Study 2b evidence above.  No storm-bed or field arm; the FIELD question this row does NOT answer is a **1 ms (299 792 m)** receiver step, five decades above the ≈3.1 m settled gate, which would trip it everywhere.
### `network.raim_erase_band_scope`  (all | min_keep_one)
- **default:** "all" = byte-identical shipped behaviour - MAIN 5fee8cb (merged 2026-09-10 18:56; ledger rows 2026-09-10 19:05) (branch worktree-evharness 46284b0 + 798a2e6, 2026-09-10).  Loader src/core/config/GnssConfigLoadNetwork.cpp (jval network.raim_erase_band_scope, hard error on any other string); enum NetworkRaimEraseBandScope + field raim_erase_band_scope in include/config/GnssConfigTypes.hpp; decision networkRaimEraseBandScope() (unit-tested) applied in NetworkRaim.cpp refloat_sat_at ~:825-885 on the per-(station, satellite, SIGNAL) `cells` of Pass 1 (the attribution's own input one aggregation step before cell_max); counters n_bscope_t4_cells / _single_band / _kept; run-end clause 'Network (band-scoped responses, §Z)' in NetworkTeardown.cpp; 22 tests in tests/test_band_scoped_response.cpp (shared with the refloat knob).  — **regime:** `on("network.raim_attribution") and on("network.phase_leveling")`.  **KEEP_OFF.**  When the T4 attribution tier erases an ISOLATED-ARC (statistical) cell, free every band EXCEPT the one with the smallest |r/sigma0| and keep that band's carried level pinned (if every band stands above k*sigma0 the pin is the least-BAD band, counted separately).  MECHANISM (docs/SPEC_event_harness.md §9 Study 1, §Z Study 3): freeing all three bands strips the LOS's last level anchor, so (rho, I, N1..3) re-draw from one epoch of code and the arc keeps a ~0.45-cyc level error for the rest of the hour that no post-fit screen can see (0 re-flags); one pinned band keeps the LOS's (range, iono) split anchored and the residual is that of a correctly handled real slip.  The T4 erase population is overwhelmingly NOT slipped (the isolated-arc refloat population is false-alarm-dominated, SPEC_slip_relevel_walk §6), so keeping a band there costs nothing and saves the re-level - the asymmetry that makes this knob armable where the detector-side scope is not.
- **evidence:** HARNESS (Study 3, each arm vs its OWN null, level floor +14..+45 after an injected T4 erase of a healthy arc): S21000SIM/G10 14 deg 0.445 -> 0.049 cyc (x9), S50000SIM/G31 81 deg 0.130 -> 0.002 (x63), S40000SIM/G31 14 deg 0.375 -> 0.013 (x29); slant-iono displacement at +45 -0.083 -> +0.006 m; 0 post-event T4/T3 re-flags in every leg.  STORM BED, T4-ONLY arm runs/golden_sim_campaign_zwdblk_bscope_t4 (this knob alone on the in-block ZWD posture, 6 h slipped 60-sta bed runs/simnet60_campaign_wu93, 720/720 epochs, 1256.6 s) vs control runs/golden_sim_campaign_zwdblk (in-block ZWD control: ZWD settled truth rms 1.188 m, consumer settled h3-h6 p50 1.084 / rms 2.389 m, ambient chord-median |e| 1.74 cyc, T4 events 9,871, ref-free WL 86.2 % / EWL 99.2 %): ZWD settled truth rms 0.845 m; consumer settled p50 / rms 2.173 / 2.644 m; ambient chord-median |e| 2.92 cyc; T4 events 9,144; ref-free WL 58.1 % / EWL 88.6 %.  VERDICT of the T4-only arm: PARTIAL on ZWD (-29 %, hourly 1.96 1.30 0.89 0.63 0.83 1.03 vs 1.98 1.31 0.96 0.58 1.05 1.67), FAIL on the consumer (p50 2.0x WORSE, rms 2.39 -> 2.64), the ambient chord-median level error (1.66 -> 2.92 cyc), the integers (ref-free WL 86.2 -> 58.1 %, EWL 99.2 -> 88.6 %, L1C 44.0 -> 29.3 %) and the T4 count (only -7 %); the satellite-clock re-realization IMPROVES (CLK-R drained 41.5 -> 27.1 m, max |A_c| 25.6 -> 10.7 m) and the T3 consensus flags rise (13,450 -> 16,580).  So the T4 scope ALONE reproduces the combined arm's collapse - the detector-side fallbacks were NOT the cause.  WHY the harness win does not transfer: on the quiet harness bed the erased arc was HEALTHY, so any pinned band carried a correct level; on the storm bed 4,400 of 9,144 cells (48 %) had EVERY band over the gate, the census puts the erased arcs at 1.9-2.2x the quiet arcs' |e_pre| (T4 finds real damage about half the time), and the pinned band is chosen by the post-fit RESIDUAL, which Study 1 showed is BLIND to a level error absorbed into the (rho, I) split (0 re-flags on a 7.7-cycle error).  Pinning by residual therefore pins a WRONG level about half the time on a bed whose ambient level error is already 1.7-2.9 cycles, and each wrong pin is a permanent injection into the LOS.  The band to keep must be chosen on evidence that CAN see a level error - the lane integers (EWL-MW / WL consistency) - i.e. the lane-preserving refloat build (SPEC_lane_slip_response §7), not a residual rank.  The mechanism (Study 1/3) stands; this implementation of the cure is falsified at storm scale.  COMBINED arm (this knob + refloat_band_scope: implicated, runs/golden_sim_campaign_zwdblk_bscope): ZWD 0.835 m (-30 %) but consumer p50 2.89 / rms 3.01, ambient 2.97 cyc, WL 56.9 % - the detector-side half's 1,091 all-band fallbacks, not this knob (SPEC_event_harness §Z.6).
- **interlock:** Only reaches the ISOLATED-ARC (statistical) T4 erase; whole-satellite T4 erases and every detector refloat are untouched.  Requires raim_attribution and phase_leveling; a cell with fewer than two carried bands has nothing to scope and falls through to the shipped erase (counted).  Not fingerprinted (a policy on the erase, no carried state changes shape) - legal on a warm resume.  Byte-identical at 'all' (products.txt 3bf7831b... on _golden_sim_off60_bscope).  Read the run-end clause: 'N cell(s) -> M band(s) erased / N kept' proves the wiring; 0 kept = inert.
### `network.cycle_slip.anchor_slip_handover`
- **default:** false = byte-identical shipped behaviour (main 40cd832, merged 2026-09-10; card ec26de1 on worktree-anchorslip).  Loader src/core/config/GnssConfigLoadNetwork.cpp (cycle_slip block; disarmed with a warning unless the prioritized anchor election runs); seam networkStageAnchorSlipHandovers in src/core/filter/NetworkPhaseDatum.cpp (reads relevel.free_ambs + carry_slipped_ambs, one directive per (sat,signal) into NetworkInjectState::anchor_now_slip; anchorFor consults the injector's own directives first); call sited immediately before networkElectPhaseAnchors in ApplicationNetwork.cpp; counters n_anc_slip_{staged,handed,continuous,cold,no_successor,inert,epochs}; PHASE_ANCHOR/anchor_slip_handover rows (station = retired incumbent, detail to=<successor>;branch=continuous|cold); run-end clause NetworkTeardown.cpp; 12 tests tests/test_anchor_slip_handover.cpp.  — **regime:** `on("network.phase_leveling") and on("network.phase_datum_prioritized")`.  **RECOMMEND_ON.**  When a slip response (MW / LLI / IF-TD drain, or the T4 attribution erase on carry_invariant lineages) FREES the arc that currently anchors a (sat, signal) phase-bias family, stage the injector's one-epoch candidacy ban for that family in CONTINUOUS mode, so the election held later in the SAME epoch hands the family to a successor pinned at the successor's own carried value_abs — before the freed arc's level is re-drawn.  MECHANISM (docs/SPEC_event_harness.md §12.6-12.7, §12.10-12.12): a phase-bias family is realized at ONE station's arc; when that arc is freed and re-levelled with the anchor unmoved (0 of 35 anchor-arc slips on the 60-station dose), the family is re-realized from the young arc — the satellite's emitted clock steps by metres, every other station's arcs of that satellite are erased as 'inconsistent', and the network walks (the storm-bed plateau, from a converged start, within 30 epochs).  Stations that anchor nothing never cascade (27 slips: 4 mm ZWD); stations anchoring 18 families cascade alone with 14-15 slips; the clock datum is exonerated (sum-to-zero seed identical).
- **evidence:** STORM BED, cold start, 6 h x 60 stations, the slipped campaign fixture (runs/golden_sim_campaign_zwdblk_ancho = the in-block control + this knob, exe 204736F4..., 1006 s, 720/720, 2026-09-10): settled ZWD truth rms 1.188 -> 0.046 m (gate <= 0.5 PASS; the quiet golden's ZWD reads 0.0072 m -- its 0.076 is the consumer rms; mislabel corrected 2026-09-11); per-station ZWD span p50 0.207 -> 0.031 m, stations with span > 1 m 15 -> 0; consumer settled p50 1.085 -> 0.278 m (gate <= 0.6 PASS), rms 2.389 -> 0.761; T4 attribution events 9,871 -> 306 (-97 %), T4 ambient per-band DD error 1.6 -> 0.26 cyc; ref-free WL 86.2 -> 98.1 %, EWL 99.2 -> 99.8 %, L1C settled 44.0 -> 81.1 %; CLK-R drained 41.5/25.6 -> 4.2/0.3 m; detector events 4,545 (unchanged); 261 families handed over (211 continuous / 50 cold fallback).  HARNESS (SPEC_event_harness s12.7, s12.11-12.12): single +40-cyc slip on an anchor arc: T4 157 -> 0, station clock 10.7 m -> 0.03 m, satellite emitted clock 9.7 m -> 3 mm; datum-station 13-slip schedule: ZWD 10.2 m -> 0.055 m, T4 352 -> 11; 60-station 655-slip dose: T4 3,099 -> 200, decouples 258 -> 44, wire 5.96 -> 0.04 m, consumer +1.46 -> -0.13 m (the 200 are one station whose anchor arc took a slip no detector caught: L5Q at 20 deg, no LLI - detector-coverage item, SPEC s12.12).  OFF byte-identical on main (null legs f3f37bc3... on the worktree exe and on main's rebuilt exe).  QUIET-BED COST, measured 2026-09-11 (24 h twins on today's golden, main's exe, both slip cards on vs both off; runs/golden_sim_quiet24_bothcards vs runs/golden_sim_quiet24_nocards): ZWD 0.0073 m both; consumer settled h12-24 rms 0.0761 → 0.0895 m (+18 %), p50 0.0603 → 0.0593, UP rms 0.0643 → 0.0768; T4 60 → 66.  The evidence refloat admitted 0 detections in those 24 h, so the tail cost belongs to the anchor-slip handover's 6 false-alarm family moves -- an OPEN cost; 60-epoch harness legs at those six epochs are owed.
- **interlock:** Response knob: it changes which station realizes a family from the slip epoch on, never a carried value's meaning, so a snapshot written OFF resumes ON and vice versa.  Inert where no family is anchored (single-pivot PB gauge) — the loader disarms it there with a warning.  A cold fallback (no usable successor carry) is COUNTED (n_anc_slip_cold) and traced, never hidden; 13 of 120 on the 60-station dose, all harmless (<= 21 mm ZWD at those stations).
- **armed when:** `on("network.cycle_slip.anchor_slip_handover")`  phase_leveling lineages with the prioritized per-(sat,signal) anchor election (the sim golden; the field golden once its leveling posture lands).  The engine it removes is a property of the anchor election, not of the bed: any lineage whose phase-bias gauge is realized at one station's arc re-realizes that family whenever a slip response frees the arc.  Knob-on legs must be graded against a knob-on null (the warm-resume refloat burst frees anchor arcs too: 16 handovers at +1 on the quiet seed).

### `network.cycle_slip.mw_evidence_refloat`
- **default:** false = byte-identical shipped behaviour (main 2490087, merged 2026-09-11; card 0550b58 on worktree agent-a6f9fa95a0c56d6f3).  Loader src/core/config/GnssConfigLoadNetwork.cpp (cycle_slip block; disarmed with a warning unless network.cycle_slip.mw_detect); detector export MwSlipResult::{evidence_ratio, arc_epochs, bia_corrected} populated at every flag site of MwSlipDetector::processMwSample; admission in PASS A (src/core/app/ApplicationNetworkAssembly.cpp, both candidate sites) — legacy rule OR the evidence rule; PassABuffers::MwRefloatCand carries integer_known / evidence_ratio / arc_epochs / via_evidence; the drain (src/core/slip/NetworkSlipQc.cpp) skips the bridge intercepts for an unknown integer and appends ';src=evidence;ratio=;n=;int=known|unknown' to the T0_mw_detect/mw_wl_slip row; always-on counters mw_drop_loose / mw_drop_unknown / mw_evidence_admitted / mw_evidence_would_admit (NetworkServiceCounters) and the run-end clause 'MW detect-only admission: legacy N, evidence N [knob on|off; would-admit N]; dropped loose N / unknown N' (NetworkTeardown.cpp); 10 tests tests/test_mw_evidence_refloat.cpp.  — **regime:** `on("network.phase_leveling") and on("network.cycle_slip.mw_detect")`.  **RECOMMEND_ON.**  Admit a Melbourne-Wübbena detection to the network's detect-only REFLOAT path on the detector's own evidence — the arc is mature (arc_epochs >= network.cycle_slip.mw_evidence_min_arc_epochs, 10), the flag is strong (|dev|/threshold >= network.cycle_slip.mw_evidence_ratio_min, 3.0) and the lane is bias-free (undifferenced, not the BIA-corrected between-satellite lane) — regardless of whether its wide-lane integer rounds cleanly.  MECHANISM (docs/SPEC_event_harness.md §13): PASS A re-used the T0 REPAIR gate (integer known AND rounding residual <= turbo_edit_frac_max 0.15 cycles) on a path whose only consumer is a refloat that never uses the integer; on the 5.86 m L2W/L5Q lane 0.15 cycles is 0.88 m, below the lane's own 1σ code noise at 20°, so the detector's correct 99-cycle flag at 78× its threshold was dropped with no counter and no event row.  Census: every band ± receiver flag caught 100 % except unflagged GPS L5 (5 of ~130, 4 %), all five the same shape (integer right, residual 0.17–0.23 cyc), all five caught by the detector, all five dropped by the gate.

Harness legs at GOPE00CZE (seed ep0604): knob-on == knob-off bit-for-bit on the quiet null and on a -99-cycle unflagged L5X slip (the legacy gate admits it on the field: lane sigma ~5x below the sim's).  No anchor election on this lineage (eq34 datum), so an admission on a datum-realizing arc costs one declared discontinuity, not a handover.  SPEC_event_harness s20.1.  STORM BED, cold start, 6 h x 60 stations, the slipped campaign fixture (runs/golden_sim_campaign_zwdblk_mwevid = the anchor-handover arm + this knob, exe 8A9BEBA1..., 1130 s, 720/720, 2026-09-11) against the anchor-handover arm of §12.13: settled ZWD truth rms 0.046 -> 0.0071 m (gate <= 0.05 PASS; = the quiet golden's own ZWD floor 0.0072 m.  QUIET NEUTRALITY, 24 h with both slip cards, runs/golden_sim_quiet24_bothcards: ZWD 0.0073 m, consumer settled rms 0.0895 / p50 0.0593 / UP 0.0768 m vs the older in-block quiet arm's 0.0875 / 0.0525 / 0.0759; T4 66; 0 evidence admissions in 24 h -- the matched no-card twin is owed for the exact delta); per-station ZWD span p50 0.031 -> 0.021 m, stations > 1 m 0 -> 0; consumer settled p50 0.278 -> 0.174 m (gate <= 0.25 PASS), rms 0.761 -> 0.365; consumer hourly rms 1.12/0.34/0.33/0.22/1.04/0.78 -> 0.62/0.29/0.23/0.18/0.47/0.39 (the hour-5/6 bump halved); T4 attribution events 306 -> 78 (-75 %), ambient 0.26 -> 0.23 cyc; ref-free WL 98.1 -> 98.9 %, EWL 99.8 -> 100.0 %, L1C 81.1 -> 87.1 %; CLK-R drained 4.2 -> 3.6 m; MW admissions legacy 4,570 + evidence 2,278 (rows 1,020: 348 integer-known, 672 unknown; ~170/h flat). QUIET-BED COST, measured 2026-09-11 (24 h twins on today's golden, main's exe, both slip cards on vs both off; runs/golden_sim_quiet24_bothcards vs runs/golden_sim_quiet24_nocards): ZWD 0.0073 m both; consumer settled h12-24 rms 0.0761 -> 0.0895 m (+18 %), p50 0.0603 -> 0.0593, UP rms 0.0643 -> 0.0768; T4 60 -> 66.  The evidence refloat admitted 0 detections in those 24 h, so the tail cost belongs to the anchor-slip handover's 6 false-alarm family moves -- an OPEN cost; 60-epoch harness legs at those six epochs are owed.
- **interlock:** Detection-side admission knob: it changes which detections reach the refloat drain, never a carried value's meaning, so a snapshot written OFF resumes ON and vice versa.  Inert without the MW detector (the loader disarms it with a warning).  On a PINNED ANCHOR arc the refloat alone is an exact solve no-op (SPEC_event_harness §13.5: freeing a pinned arc's ambiguities, 3 or 2 bands, reads 0.0000 on every channel — the pin keeps its level), so on such arcs the knob only supplies the detection and network.cycle_slip.anchor_slip_handover is the cure; arm the two together (both are pinned in the sim golden).  Two MW pairs firing on one satellite free all three bands although only their common band slipped (the lane-intersection scope is the open follow-up, SPEC §13.6).  The OFF run's run-end clause reports 'would-admit N' — a nonzero count on an OFF lineage names the population this knob would have refloated.
- **armed when:** `on("network.cycle_slip.mw_evidence_refloat")`  phase_leveling lineages running the MW detector (the sim golden).  Arm together with network.cycle_slip.anchor_slip_handover where a prioritized anchor election exists: on an anchor arc this knob supplies the detection and the handover supplies the cure.  Knob-on legs against a knob-on null (the quiet null is byte-identical either way, but a dosed leg is not).  the field golden (golden_field_gps_dual) PINS it since 2026-09-12: 6 h field seed OFF vs twin ON (runs/evseed_field6h / runs/evseed_field6h_ev, exe 3FDC5F8D = main 109a6b2, 720/720 emitted and 0 withheld in both): on the field the L5 MW lanes are 0 % BIA-corrected (L1C/L2W 75 %), i.e. they ARE the bias-free population this rule admits; the OFF run's would-admit = 10 in 6 h; ON admitted 10 lane detections on 5 mature L5-lane arcs (BOGT/G32, BSHM/G30, KIT3/G06, SYOG/G26, CHUR/G03; ratio 4.5-7.7; 3 with an integer the legacy gate can never admit); satellite clocks <= 3 mm ON-OFF at every epoch; the SYOG/G26 admission re-realized the (G26,L5X) phase-bias datum by 9.2 m in the admission epoch and it is DECLARED (disc_counter 0->1); consumer WTZZ (L1/L2 float) pb_reset/cb_disc/clk_disc identical 29/590/6, stepping-epoch fraction identical 0.1181 (p90 of the per-epoch max emitted step 0.127->0.257 m, recorded), de-biased settled h3-6 p50 5.28->5.49 m (+4.0 %) and rms 6.48->6.16 (-5 %).  Harness legs at GOPE00CZE (seed ep0604): knob-on == knob-off bit-for-bit on the quiet null and on a -99-cycle unflagged L5X slip (the legacy gate admits it on the field: lane sigma ~5x below the sim's).  No anchor election on this lineage (eq34 datum), so an admission on a datum-realizing arc costs one declared discontinuity, not a handover.  SPEC_event_harness s20.1.

### `network.cycle_slip.common_band_scope`
- **default:** false = byte-identical shipped behaviour (main 8f6ee5b, merged 2026-09-11; card 5fe64c7 + 012ff2d + 5223dc6 on worktree agent-af38ad6be8a0d068b).  Loader src/core/config/GnssConfigLoadNetwork.cpp (cycle_slip block; disarmed with a warning unless network.cycle_slip.mw_detect); verdict seam include/slip/NetworkCommonBandScope.hpp + src/core/slip/NetworkCommonBandScope.cpp called before networkSlipDrainMwRefloat (src/core/app/ApplicationNetwork.cpp); detector exports the per-epoch pair status (arc count, flagged, threshold) from MwSlipDetector::processMwSample; the IF-TD driver's pooled centre / scale / shrinkage factored into ifTdPoolStats + ifTdShrunkScale (include/slip/NetworkSlipQc.hpp) so the verdict's read-only IF-TD flag preview cannot drift from the decision; drains free (their bands ∩ implicated); rows T0_band_scope/common_band (keep = narrowed: implicated=…;exonerated=…;basis=…; all = fallback with reason no_exoneration | inconsistent | anchor_colocate); counters cb_verdicts / cb_narrowed / cb_bands_kept / cb_all_no_exoneration / cb_all_inconsistent / cb_anchor_colocate / cb_iftd_veto / cb_immature_pairs / cb_weak_silence and the ALWAYS-ON split_referenced_sats mean / max in the run-end clause; 27 tests tests/test_common_band_scope.cpp; scripts/event_leg.py --extra.  — **regime:** `on("network.phase_leveling") and on("network.cycle_slip.mw_detect")`.  **RECOMMEND_ON.**  When Melbourne-Wübbena pairs flag a satellite at a station, free only the bands the evidence NAMES: a pair that the detector evaluated this epoch, whose arc count is >= network.cycle_slip.common_band_min_arc_epochs (10) and which did NOT flag is SILENT and exonerates both its bands — unless the IF-TD detector flagged that pair this epoch (the equal-count trap: L1 and L2 slipping by the same integer leave MW(1,2) silent but move the iono-free combination 0.107 m per cycle) — and a band with a loss-of-lock flag is always implicated.  Implicated = bands no silent pair vouches for; one verdict per (station, satellite, epoch) governs every drain (MW refloat, LLI free, IF-TD pair refloat), so the per-epoch union is exactly the implicated set.  ANCHOR CLAUSE: on an arc that anchors ANY family of the satellite the verdict never narrows (reason anchor_colocate; all bands freed so the satellite's families move together to one successor).  Fallbacks to today's all-band response: no pair fired, nothing exonerated, or an inconsistent verdict (a fired pair whose bands are all exonerated).  MECHANISM (docs/SPEC_event_harness.md §14): a single-band slip fires every pair containing the band, and the drain refloats each firing pair's two bands, so the union is all three — the §9 all-band floor (0.45–0.65 cyc for 45 epochs at low elevation, slant iono −0.08 m) for a one-band event; on the storm bed ~2.2 admissions per detection row, i.e. most responses were all-band.  RULE F2 / RULE F3 (s14.11 / s14.12) added 2026-09-11 pm -- see the JSON row scope.
- **evidence:** FP-1 (SPEC_event_harness s20.2, 2026-09-13; the s20 matrix re-run on the warm-resuming field seed evseed_field6h_v5 ep0604, legs runs/evleg_fp1_*): G-A reproduces every pre-registered row (E1 {L5X}; E2 all = the clause's cost; E3/G24 all via ref_arc = the trap closed; E3/G25 all via the (1,5) veto) and the G25 control is clean, BUT if_phase_td_pairs=all fans a slip ON a chain's reference satellite out to every other satellite of that chain (G24 = both L5 chains' reference at GOPE: E1 six false refloats on G11/G25/G32 at 253-1580x the threshold, an innocent satellite's phase bias re-realized network-wide by 13.7 m, consumer mean |d3D| 4.71 vs 0.98 m; no backstop - card R tests the first chain only, T4 = 0, no scope row to narrow) => G-B/G-C/G-D FAIL on every G24 leg; NOT pinned.  Counter-weight: at +39 a real 46-sigma (1,5) slip at JPLM/G10 is caught by all and missed by first (pair-rotation pool restart).  The fix is REF-FAN (s22.6, network.cycle_slip.if_phase_td_ref_arm_all); the pin is re-run once it passes F1-F4.  FIELD HARNESS (SPEC_event_harness s20, 2026-09-12): FIELD EXCEPTION -- field legs at GOPE00CZE (seed ep0604, 60 epochs, event at +10): single-band slips narrow correctly on real data (E1 L5X -99 -> {L5X}; E2 L1C +37 -> {L1C}; E5 L5X -31 with LLI -> {L5X} via rule F3), but the equal-count L1C+L5X +29 slip on G24 narrowed to the INNOCENT band L2W under BOTH if_phase_td_pairs first and all, because G24 is the IF-TD reference satellite of both L5 chains at that station and a reference arc is UNTESTED in the additional chains (its deviation is ref-minus-ref = 0.000000 by construction; 41 such arcs network-wide = exactly one per station, the highest L5-capable satellite in 80.6 % of epochs, rank <= 2 in 99 %).  At G25 (not the reference) pairs=all vetoes exactly (iftd(L1C+L5X)=flagged, 3.15 m vs 0.109 m) and pairs=first keeps L2W.  On carry_invariant the wrong narrowing has no T4 backstop (attribution 0 for 6 h, RAIM-pool re-host gap) and its wire/consumer cost could not be priced: the field golden cannot warm-resume (61/61 epochs withheld at the seam, SH a00 -4810 TECU; Card 1f carry_coupling_gauge_project is the loader-named prerequisite, fingerprinted, absent from the golden - owed as its own card).  FIX STUB before a field pin: a pair whose IF-TD chain holds the satellite as its reference is NOT silent for the verdict (untested => immature => all-band on that arc), re-priced on G24 and G25; then pin together with if_phase_td_pairs = all on a seed that warm-resumes.  SPEC_event_harness s20.1. HARNESS (SPEC_event_harness §14.2–14.8, worker build on its worktree, manager re-graded): G1 OFF byte-identical to main's exe on every iteration and on main's own rebuild after the merge (7302422E…); single-band legs at S21 (anchors nothing) narrow to exactly the slipped band — L5 → {L5Q}, L1 → {L1C}, L2 → {L2W}, LLI on L5 → {L5Q}; per-band level residual at +14 / +45 24–300× below the OFF twin, slant-iono step 0.15 m → 2 mm, other arcs' rms 0.0170 → 0.00013 cyc; controls: a genuine three-band slip → all (products byte-identical to OFF), equal-count L1+L2 → all via the IF-TD veto exactly once; a (1,2) arc 8 samples old refused (immature) as specified; the S36/G04 anchor slip: without the clause ONE family handed over (the satellite split), with it all THREE to the same successor; 655-slip dose: per-slip level residual p50 0.381 → 0.033 / 0.201 → 0.036 / 0.151 → 0.030 cyc at +1 / +20 / +45, injected-arc pool residual 0.51 → 0.04, split-referenced satellites 4.79/7 per epoch = the no-scope baseline to two decimals (iteration 1 10.6, iteration 2 17.0), handovers 138 = baseline, consumer the best of four arms (ends 0.294 m vs 0.307 baseline), T4 0, detector rows identical; quiet 240-ep null products byte-identical. A narrow lane's silence excludes only slips above its own threshold — on the sim the L1/L2 and L1/L5 lanes run 3–11 WL cycles (code noise ~5× the field's); 22 % of exonerating silences on the dose sit on gates above 6 WL (cb_weak_silence); with three lanes plus IF-TD a single missed lane yields an INCONSISTENT verdict (every band sits in two lanes) and falls back to all, so a wrong narrowing needs two lanes to miss the same slip — the storm bed's T4 count is the guard.  ⚠ Do NOT narrow on anchor arcs without the clause: iteration 2 (no clause) split a satellite's references across stations on 44 % of satellite-epochs and cost the user (SPEC §14.5–14.7).
